. The conditions given in the statement of the proposition ensure that this happens. For example, if
v_{1}\in T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
but
v_{1}\notin T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
, then
v_{1}\notin{\,\rm e}\,^{B}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
. It follows that
v_{1}
cannot be in
{\,\rm e}\,^{B}(K_{2}^{\perp})
. The only way this can happen is if
\iota_{v_{1}}B_{\rm mix}\in T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
and is non-zero. Since this happens for all
v_{1}\in T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap N{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
,
B_{\rm mix}
gives an isomorphism between
T{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap N{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
and
N^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
. The other conditions follow from similar considerations.
∎

We shall now consider the setting of Example
5.26
, since it is our main source of explicit examples.

Theorem 5.94
.

Let
(\mathbb{T}M_{1},H_{1})
and
(\mathbb{T}M_{2},H_{2})
be twisted standard Courant algebroids as in Example
5.26
and Proposition
5.27
, isomorphic via
\Phi=\overline{\varphi}\circ{\,\rm e}\,^{B}\,
, and suppose that
B
decomposes as in Equation (
5.87
). Take a generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
defined by
\underline{g}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
\underline{b}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{2}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and define the subalgebras
\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})
and
\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})
by

(5.95)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})=

\displaystyle\set{\underline{X}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(D_{1})\colon\pounds_{\underline{X}}(\underline{g}_{1}+\underline{b}_{1})=0}\,,

(5.96)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})=

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1})^{-1}_{*}(\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+}))\cap{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\,.

Suppose that
\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})
spans pointwise
D_{1}
, and that for each
X\in\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})

(5.97)

\displaystyle\pounds_{X}B=0\,.

Then there exists a unique generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
such that
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},H_{1},V_{1}^{+})
and
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},H_{2},V_{2}^{+})
are geometrically T-dual, i.e.
R
is a generalised isometry between
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
.

Proof.

By Remarks
5.30
and
5.42
, the conditions (
5.97
) ensure that the generalised metric
(\underline{g}{}_{1},\underline{b}{}_{1})
is
D_{1}
-invariant and the adapted splittings are compatible. The decomposition (
5.87
) ensures that conditions
(i)
and
(ii)
of Theorem
5.46
are satisfied. Hence the conclusion follows by Theorem
5.46
.
∎

Remark 5.98
.

The condition
\pounds_{X}B=0
for
X\in\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})
together with the assumption of reducibility of
E
with respect to
K_{2}
tells us that

(5.99)

\displaystyle\iota_{X}H_{1}=\iota_{X}\,\mathrm{d}B=-\mathrm{d}(\iota_{X}B)\ ,

similarly to Equation (
1.5
), i.e. it gives additional symmetries of the sigma-model Wess-Zumino functional for
M,
as discussed in Section
1.1
.

Remark 5.100
.

To see why we named
D_{1}
the distribution of T-duality directions, consider a point
(q_{1},q_{2})\in C\subset{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
, and take
\underline{v}\in(D_{1})_{q_{1}}
. Let
v\in\big(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\big)_{m}
be such that
\varpi_{1*}(v)=\underline{v}
, where
\varpi_{1}(m)=q_{1}
. Denote
\varpi_{2}(m)=q_{2}
. It follows that
v\in(T_{m}{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\cap\mathrm{Ann}^{*}(T_{m}{\mathcal{F}}_{1})).
By Proposition
5.86
we have

(5.101)

\displaystyle\iota_{v}B=\iota_{v}B_{\rm mix}\ \in\ T_{m}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\cap\mathrm{Ann}(T_{m}{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\ .

Thus the corresponding element in
R_{(q_{1},q_{2})}
is

(5.102)

\displaystyle\big(\natural_{1}(v),\natural_{2}(v)\big)=\big(\underline{v},\natural_{2}(\iota_{v}B)\big)\ .

This is the usual exchange of tangent and cotangent (momentum and winding) directions seen in T-duality.

6.
T-duality Relations and Doubled Geometry

In this Section we will show how the construction of Section
5
describes the geometry of various kinds of T-duality such as T-duality with a correspondence space, where the Buscher rules for torus bundles are perfectly reproduced by the generalised isometry of exact Courant algebroids, and generalised T-duality of doubled sigma-models whose target space is endowed with an almost para-Hermitian structure. In particular, we discuss explicitly the Buscher rules for arbitrary rank torus bundles, as well as the examples of T-duality for lens spaces in three dimensions and generalised T-duality for the doubled Heisenberg nilmanifold in six dimensions.

6.1.
T-duality for Correspondence Spaces

We briefly recall the definition of T-duality from
[
8
]
for principal torus bundles, and show that this nicely fits into our definition.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
be principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-bundles over a common base manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
, and let
{\underline{H}}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{3}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
{\underline{H}}{}_{2}\in\mathsf{\Omega}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}^{3}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
be
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant closed three-forms. Consider the fibred product
M\coloneqq{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
giving the correspondence space diagram

(6.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varpi_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{2}}

with
M
endowed with the closed three-form
\varpi_{1}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{1}-\varpi_{2}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{2}.

Then
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are
T-dual
if there is a
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-invariant two-form
B\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{2}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}}(M)
such that

(6.3)

\displaystyle\varpi_{1}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{1}-\varpi_{2}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{2}=\mathrm{d}B\ ,

and the smooth skew-symmetric map

(6.4)

\displaystyle B\colon\mathfrak{k}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{k}_{2}\longrightarrow\mathbb{R}

is non-degenerate, where
\mathfrak{k}_{i}=\ker(\varpi_{i*})
for
i=1,2
.

Remark 6.5

(
Correspondence Spaces as Bisubmersions
)
.

The commutative diagram (
6.2
) represents a particular case of a bisubmersion, as discussed in Remark
5.21
. The condition (
5.22
) is clearly satisfied, since the generators of the
\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{1}
-action commute with the generators of the
\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2}
-action, where
{\sf Lie}({\mathsf{T}}^{k})=\mathfrak{t}^{k}
and the subscript
i
indicates the manifold
{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}
on which
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
acts. Here the
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-action whose orbits are the integral manifolds of
\ker(\varpi_{2*})
induces a
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-action on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
which, in order to provide Definition
6.1
, is supposed to be free and proper. The distribution induced by this
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-action on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
defines the distribution of T-duality directions
D_{1}=(\varpi_{1})_{*}\mathfrak{k}_{2}
, whose integral manifolds are the fibres of
\pi_{1}\colon{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{B}}
.
Similarly,
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
inherits a
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-action, and hence a distribution
D_{2}
, whose foliation, given by the fibres of
\pi_{2}\colon{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{B}},
is Hausdorff Morita equivalent to the foliation induced by
D_{1}
.

Let us reinterpret this picture in the setting of Section
5
. We start with

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
be T-dual in the sense of Definition
6.1
. Then there is a T-duality relation in the sense of Definition
5.3
.

Proof.

It is enough to find a Courant algebroid
E
over
M
such that Definition
5.3
is satisfied.
Take with
E=(\mathbb{T}M,\,H=\varpi_{1}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{1})
over
M={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{\mathcal{B}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
along with
K_{1}=\mathfrak{k}_{1}
and
K_{2}={\,\rm e}\,^{-B}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})
.
Since
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are T-dual in the sense of Definition
6.1
, there exists
B\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{2}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}}(M)
satisfying Equation (
6.3
) which is non-degenerate on
\mathfrak{k}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{k}_{2}
.
Moreover,
{\,\rm e}\,^{-B}\,(\mathfrak{k}_{2})\cap\mathfrak{k}_{1}=\set{0}
has constant rank. Hence we may form the Courant algebroid relation
R
fitting the diagram

(6.7)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(K_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(K_{1})^{\top}}

which is supported on
M={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{{\mathcal{B}}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
by Lemma
5.8
.
∎

Note that the converse is not true, since our notion of T-duality related Courant algebroids imposes no condition on
B
other than the constant rank condition from Proposition
5.27
, which is trivially satisfied for any two-form
B\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{2}(M)
since
\mathfrak{k}_{1}\cap\mathfrak{k}_{2}=\set{0}
.

We will see that, upon introducing geometric data, the possible choices for
B
coincide with the
B
-field choice in Definition
6.1
. This is exemplified through

Proposition 6.8
.

If
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are T-dual in the sense of Definition
6.1
, and there is a
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}_{1}
-invariant generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
on
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})
, then there is a unique generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
such that
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},{\underline{H}}{}_{1},V_{1}^{+})
and
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},{\underline{H}}{}_{2},V_{2}^{+})
are geometrically T-dual.

Proof.

If
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
are T-dual in the sense of Definition
6.1
, then
B
decomposes as in Equation (
5.87
). Furthermore, since
V_{1}^{+}
is
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant, we define
17
17

17

If a Lie group
{\mathsf{G}}
acts on a manifold
M
, we denote by
\#_{M}\colon\mathfrak{g}\to{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(TM)
the corresponding action of its Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
on
M
.

\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})\coloneqq\#_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}(\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2})\subset{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(D_{1})
, which spans
D_{1}
pointwise. Thus
\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})=\#_{M}(\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2})\subset{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})
, and since
B
is
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-invariant it follows that
\pounds_{X}B=0,
for any
X\in\mathsf{Iso}(W_{1})
. The conclusion then follows by Theorem
5.94
.
∎

In this picture, we can also provide a further characterisation of the components of the three-form
\underline{H}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}_{\rm cl}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
through

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})
and
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})
be twisted standard Courant algebroids with
\underline{H}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
\underline{H}{}_{2}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
, and let them be endowed with generalised metrics
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
such that they are geometrically T-dual. Then
\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}
can be written as the sum of a doubly pulled back three-form
H_{\mathcal{B}}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}({\mathcal{B}})
and a component determined by

(6.10)

\displaystyle\iota_{X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}}\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}=\iota_{X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}}\,\mathrm{d}B\ ,

for any
X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2})
.

Proof.

Definition
6.1
implies that
\iota_{Y_{\texttt{v}_{1}}}\,\iota_{X_{\texttt{v}_{1}}}\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}=0,

for all
X_{\texttt{v}_{1}},Y_{\texttt{v}_{1}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{1}),
since
B
is
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-invariant and
\iota_{X_{\texttt{v}_{1}}}\,\varpi^{*}_{2}\,\underline{H}{}_{2}=0,
for all
X_{\texttt{v}_{1}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{1}).
Hence only two components of
\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}
are non-vanishing and, because of
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariance of
\underline{H}{}_{1},
one of them must be the double pullback of a three-form on
{\mathcal{B}}.
The other component is characterised as follows. Since any
\underline{X}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(D_{1})
lifts to a section

X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})
, and
\varpi^{*}_{2}\,\underline{H}{}_{2}
is basic with respect to the orbits of
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
whose tangent distribution is
\mathfrak{k}_{2},
that is it comes from the base
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
, Equation (
6.10
) follows.
∎

Example 6.11

(
\boldsymbol{B}
-fields from Connections
)
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
be principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-bundles over a common base manifold
{\mathcal{B}}
, and let them be endowed with closed three-forms
\underline{H}{}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
\underline{H}{}_{2}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{3}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2}).
Choose connections
\theta_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{1})
and
\theta_{2}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2}),
respectively, and suppose that

B=-\varpi^{*}_{1}\theta_{1}\wedge\varpi^{*}_{2}\theta_{2}\ .

When Lemma
6.9
holds, we can write

(6.12)

\displaystyle\varpi^{*}_{1}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}=-\varpi^{*}_{1}\theta_{1}\wedge\varpi^{*}_{2}\big(c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})\big)+\varpi^{*}_{1}(\pi^{*}_{1}H_{\mathcal{B}})\ ,

where
c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})=\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}
represents the Chern class of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}.

This can be shown by letting
X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})
be a generator of the
\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2}
-action. Then

(6.13)

\displaystyle\iota_{X_{\texttt{v}_{2}}}\varpi_{1}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{1}=\mathrm{d}(\varpi^{*}_{2}\theta_{2})=\varpi^{*}_{2}\big(c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})\big)\ ,

and Equation (
6.12
) follows.
A symmetric argument holds for
\varpi_{2}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{2}.
This is the key case considered in
[
18
,
8
]
which provides criteria for the existence of T-dual pairs based on the components of the
H
-flux. It shows how T-duality in this case is characterised by an interchange of the Chern classes of the torus bundle with topological data associated to the
H
-flux.

The analogy with the correspondence space picture extends further when we consider the construction in
[
8
, Section 3]
, which uses the Fourier-Mukai transform, of the isomorphism
18
18

18

In
[
8
]
the isomorphism is denoted by
\varphi
, but here we use the symbol
\mathscr{R}
to distinguish it from the already used
\varphi
in the present paper.
of
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant sections
\mathscr{R}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\to\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
.
In that construction a
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant section
X_{1}+\xi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
is lifted to a
{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}
-invariant section
{\hat{X}}_{1}+\varpi_{1}^{*}\xi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}}(\mathbb{T}M)
whose image under
B
-field transformation is basic:

(6.14)

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1}^{*}\xi_{1})(Y_{2})+B({\hat{X}}_{1},Y_{2})=0\ ,

for all
Y_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2})
. The non-degeneracy of
B
on
\mathfrak{k}_{1}\otimes\mathfrak{k}_{2}
ensures that the lift
{\hat{X}}_{1}
satisfying Equation (
6.14
) is unique.
The isomorphism
\mathscr{R}
is then constructed as the pushforward by
\varpi_{2}
of
{\hat{X}}_{1}+\varpi_{1}^{*}\xi_{1}+\iota_{\hat{X}_{1}}B
:

(6.15)

\displaystyle\mathscr{R}(X_{1}+\xi_{1})\coloneqq\varpi_{2*}{\hat{X}}_{1}+\varpi_{1}^{*}\xi_{1}+\iota_{\hat{X}_{1}}B\ .

In our language, since the Courant algebroid relation
R
is a generalised isometry, it follows by Lemma
5.49
that conditions
(i)
and
(ii)
of Theorem
5.46
are satisfied.
Hence by Proposition
5.56
there is a splitting
s\colon(\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}/\mathfrak{k}_{1}\to(\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}
such that
\mathrm{im}(s)\subset\big({\,\rm e}\,^{-B}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})\big)^{\perp}
.
This splitting is unique up to elements in
{\,\rm e}\,^{-B}\,(\mathfrak{k}_{2})\cap\mathfrak{k}_{1}=\set{0}
.
It follows that, at a point
(m,q_{1})\in\text{gr}(\varpi_{1})\subset M\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, an element
v_{1}+\nu_{1}\in\mathbb{T}_{q_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
lifts uniquely to an element
\hat{v}_{1}+\varpi_{1}^{*}\nu_{1}\in\mathbb{T}_{m}M
given by

(6.16)

\displaystyle\hat{v}_{1}+\varpi_{1}^{*}\nu_{1}\coloneqq s_{m}\big(\mathscr{J}_{q_{1},m}(v_{1}+\nu_{1})\big)\ ,

such that

(6.17)

\displaystyle(\varpi_{1}^{*}\nu_{1})(v_{2})+B(\hat{v}_{1},v_{2})=0\ ,

for all
v_{2}\in(\mathfrak{k}_{2})_{m}.

When we restrict this construction to
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant sections, the analogy becomes exact through

Proposition 6.18
.

The T-duality relation
R
gives rise to an isomorphism
\underline{\Phi}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\to\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
of
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-modules which coincides with the isomorphism
\mathscr{R}
defined by Equation (
6.15
).

Proof.

Recall that in this case, a basic section
\hat{\psi}_{1}=X_{1}+\xi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{bas}}\big((\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}\big)
satisfies

(6.19)

\displaystyle\llbracket Y_{1},X_{1}+\xi_{1}\rrbracket_{H}=[Y_{1},X_{1}]+\pounds_{Y_{1}}\xi_{1}\ ,

for every
Y_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})
, where
X_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{\mathsf{T}^{k}}(TM),
i.e.
X_{1}
is projectable with respect to
\mathfrak{k}_{1},
and
\xi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}({\rm Ann}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})).

Suppose that
\psi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
.
We know that
\mathscr{J}
extends to an isomorphism of
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
-modules

\mathscr{J}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\to\mathsf{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{bas}}((\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp})/\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})
.
By Proposition
5.56
, the unique splitting
s\colon(\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}/\mathfrak{k}_{1}\to(\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}
extends to a map between sections as

(6.20)

\displaystyle s\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}\bigl((\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}\bigr)\,\big/\,\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathfrak{k}_{1})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}\bigl((\mathfrak{k}_{1})^{\perp}\bigr)\ ,

since it covers the identity,
and so we may uniquely define

(6.21)

\displaystyle\hat{\psi}_{1}\coloneqq s\big(\mathscr{J}(\psi_{1})\big)\ \in\ \mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2k}}\bigl(\bigl({\,\rm e}\,^{-B}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})\bigr)^{\perp}\bigr)\ .

and it follows that
\hat{\psi}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{\mathrm{bas}}\bigl(\left({\,\rm e}\,^{-B}(\mathfrak{k}_{2})\right)^{\perp}\bigr)
. Define
\psi_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
by

(6.22)

\displaystyle\psi_{2}\coloneqq\natural_{2}\bigl(\hat{\psi}_{1}\bigr)\ .

It follows that
\psi_{1}\sim_{R}\psi_{2}
. By construction, for every
\psi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
there is a unique section
\psi_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
such that
\psi_{1}\sim_{R}\psi_{2}
. Similarly, for every
\psi_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
there is a unique section
\psi_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
such that
\psi_{2}\sim_{R^{\top}}\psi_{1}
, where
R^{\top}
is the transpose Courant algebroid relation.

Thus the map

(6.23)

\displaystyle\underline{\Phi}\colon\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})\longrightarrow\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})

defined by

(6.24)

\displaystyle\underline{\Phi}(\psi_{1})=\psi_{2}\qquad\text{with}\quad\psi_{1}\sim_{R}\psi_{2}

is well-defined and injective, hence bijective. It follows that
\underline{\Phi}
is a
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module isomorphism, where the
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{B}})
-module structures on
\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1})
and
\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
are given by pulling back smooth functions on
{\mathcal{B}}
by
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
, respectively. It is clear by construction that
\psi_{1}\sim_{R}\psi_{2}
if and only if
\mathscr{R}(\psi_{1})=\psi_{2}
, hence
\underline{\Phi}=\mathscr{R}
.
∎

Remark 6.25
.

In the construction of
\underline{\Phi}
in Proposition
6.18
there is no mention of a Fourier-Mukai integral transform as in the analogous construction of
\mathscr{R}
in
[
8
]
. Thus it may be possible to extend a correspondence space type picture for T-duality to cases of non-compact manifolds, where the Fourier-Mukai transform is not defined, such as Drinfel’d doubles. We defer this to future work.

Remark 6.26

(
Buscher Rules
)
.

We pick connections
\theta_{1}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{1})
and
\theta_{2}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2})
for the principal
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-bundles
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}.

To fulfil the conditions of Theorem
5.94
, we use Remark
6.5
to consider a
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant generalised metric
(\underline{g}{}_{1},\underline{b}{}_{1})
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, and set
\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})=\#_{1}(\mathfrak{t}_{2}^{k})
.

The connection
\theta_{i}
gives a splitting

(6.27)

\displaystyle T{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}\simeq\mathsf{Hor}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\oplus\mathsf{Ver}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})

into horizontal and vertical subbundles, for
i=1,2
, whose sections we denote by
\underline{X}^{\rm h}+\underline{X}^{{\rm v}_{i}}
. This also gives a decomposition of

(6.28)

\displaystyle\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{i}=\bigl(\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}\mathsf{Hor}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\bigr)\ \oplus\ \bigl(\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}\mathsf{Ver}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\bigr)\ \oplus\ \bigl(\mathsf{Hor}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\otimes\mathsf{Ver}^{*}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})\bigr)\ .

We denote the corresponding components of sections as
\underline{\alpha}^{\rm h}+\underline{\alpha}^{{\rm v}_{i}}+\underline{\alpha}^{{\rm m}_{i}}
.
Thus, denoting

\underline{h}{}_{1}=\underline{g}{}_{1}+\underline{b}{}_{1}\ ,

a section
\underline{w}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(V_{1}^{+})
can be written as

(6.29)

\displaystyle\underline{w}{}_{1}=\underline{X}^{{\rm v}_{1}}+\underline{X}^{\rm h}+\underline{A}_{1}\big(\underline{X}^{{\rm v}_{1}}+\underline{X}^{\rm h}\big)\ ,

where

(6.30)

\displaystyle\underline{A}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}\underline{h}{}_{1}^{{\rm v}_{1}}&\underline{h}{}_{1}^{{\rm m}_{1}}\\[3.0pt]
\underline{\tilde{h}}{}_{1}^{{\rm m}_{1}}&\underline{h}{}_{1}^{\rm h}\end{pmatrix}\qquad\text{with}\quad\underline{\tilde{h}}{}_{1}^{{\rm m}_{1}}=(\underline{h}_{1}^{{\rm m}_{1}})^{\rm t}\ .

The pullbacks of the connections
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
give a splitting

(6.31)

\displaystyle TM\simeq\mathsf{Hor}(M)\oplus\mathsf{Ver}_{1}(M)\oplus\mathsf{Ver}_{2}(M)\ ,

and we identify
\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(\mathsf{Ver}_{i}(M))\simeq\mathsf{\Gamma}(\mathsf{Ver}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i}))
since they are isomorphic as
C^{\infty}({\mathcal{Q}}_{i})
-modules.
19
19

19

This follows from the fact that there exists a fibrewise bijective map covering
\varpi_{i}
between them, see
[
38
]
.
Thus
\underline{w}_{1}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(V_{1}^{+})
lifts to
w_{1}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(W_{1})
given by

(6.32)

\displaystyle w_{1}=X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}+X^{{\rm v}_{2}}+A_{1}\big(X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}\big)\ ,

where by using the non-degenerate map
B\colon\mathfrak{k}_{1}\to(\mathfrak{k}_{2})^{*}
we write

(6.33)

\displaystyle X^{{\rm v}_{2}}=-(B^{\rm t})^{-1}\big(\bigl(A_{1}(X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X_{1}^{\rm h})\bigr)^{{\rm v}_{1}}\big)=-(B^{\rm t})^{-1}\big(h_{1}^{{\rm v}_{1}}(X^{{\rm v}_{1}})+h_{1}^{{\rm m}_{1}}(X^{\rm h})\big)\ ,

and
h_{1}
is the pullback of
\underline{h}{}_{1}
determining the
\mathfrak{t}_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}^{k}
-transverse generalised metric
W_{1}
.

We can now compute

(6.34)

\displaystyle w_{2}:={\,\rm e}\,^{B}\,(w_{1})

\displaystyle=X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}+X^{{\rm v}_{2}}+\iota_{X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}+X^{{\rm v}_{2}}}B+A_{1}\big(X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}\big)

(6.35)

\displaystyle=X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}+X^{{\rm v}_{2}}+B(X^{{\rm v}_{1}})^{{\rm v}_{2}}+B(X^{{\rm v}_{2}})^{{\rm v}_{1}}+A_{1}\big(X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}\big)

(6.36)

\displaystyle=X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h}+X^{{\rm v}_{2}}+B(X^{{\rm v}_{1}})^{{\rm v}_{2}}+\bigr(A_{1}(X^{{\rm v}_{1}}+X^{\rm h})\bigl)^{\rm h}\ ,

using
B(X^{\rm h})=0
,
B(X^{{\rm v}_{i}})^{{\rm v}_{i}}=0
, and Equation (
6.33
) for the last equality.
It follows that
w_{2}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{k}}(W_{2})
for some
\mathfrak{t}^{k}_{2}
-transverse generalised metric
W_{2}
, with

(6.37)

\displaystyle A_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}h_{2}^{\rm v_{2}}&h_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}\\[3.0pt]
\tilde{h}_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}&h_{2}^{\rm h}\end{pmatrix}\ .

This means that

(6.38)

\displaystyle B(X^{\rm v_{1}})^{\rm v_{2}}+\bigl(A_{1}\,(X^{\rm v_{1}}+X^{\rm h})\bigr)^{\rm h}=A_{2}(X^{\rm v_{2}}+X^{\rm h})\ ,

or equivalently

(6.39)

\displaystyle B(X^{\rm v_{1}})

\displaystyle=h_{2}^{\rm v_{2}}(X^{\rm v_{2}})+h_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}(X^{\rm h})\ ,

and

(6.40)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}(X^{\rm v_{1}})+h_{1}^{\rm h}(X^{\rm h})

\displaystyle=\tilde{h}_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}(X^{\rm v_{2}})+h_{2}^{\rm h}(X^{\rm h})\ .

Substituting Equation (
6.33
), we get

(6.41)

\displaystyle B(X^{\rm v_{1}})

\displaystyle=-h_{2}^{\rm v_{2}}\big((B^{\rm t})^{-1}(h_{1}^{\rm v_{1}}(X^{\rm v_{1}}))\big)\ ,

(6.42)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=-h_{2}^{\rm v_{2}}\big((B^{\rm t})^{-1}(h_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}(X^{\rm h}))\big)+h_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}(X^{\rm h})\ ,

(6.43)

\displaystyle\tilde{h}_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}(X^{\rm v_{1}})

\displaystyle=-\tilde{h}_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}\big((B^{\rm t})^{-1}(h_{1}^{\rm v_{1}}(X^{\rm v_{1}}))\big)\ ,

(6.44)

\displaystyle h_{1}^{\rm h}(X^{\rm h})

\displaystyle=-\tilde{h}_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}\big((B^{\rm t})^{-1}(h_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}(X^{\rm h}))\big)+h_{2}^{\rm h}(X^{\rm h})\ ,

yielding

(6.45)

\displaystyle h_{2}^{\rm v_{2}}

\displaystyle=-B\,(h_{1}^{\rm v_{1}})^{-1}\,B^{\rm t}\ ,

(6.46)

\displaystyle h_{2}^{\rm m_{2}}

\displaystyle=B\,(h_{1}^{\rm v_{1}})^{-1}\,h_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}\ ,

(6.47)

\displaystyle h_{2}^{\rm h}

\displaystyle=h_{1}^{\rm h}-\tilde{h}_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}\,(h_{1}^{\rm v_{1}})^{-1}\,h_{1}^{\rm m_{1}}\ .

By reducing to
\underline{h}{}_{2}=\underline{g}{}_{2}+\underline{b}{}_{2}
, one can now unravel these formulas to read off the structure tensors
\underline{g}{}_{2}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
and
\underline{b}{}_{2}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
of the corresponding
{\mathsf{T}}^{k}
-invariant generalised metric on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. This results in global Buscher rules for the transformation of the metric and Kalb-Ramond field under T-duality.

The case of an
{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
-fibration discussed in
[
8
, Section 4]
is a simplified case of the construction of Remark
6.26
, which here comes from the defining conditions for the generalised isometry
R.

Example 6.48

(
Lens Spaces
)
.

We apply the construction above to an explicit example.
Consider a three-dimensional
{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
-bundle
\pi_{1}\colon{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\to{\mathsf{S}}^{2}
. Choose a chart with coordinates
(x,y,z)
such that
z
is adapted to the circle fibres and
(x,y)
are pullback coordinates from the two-sphere
{\mathsf{S}}^{2}.
Pick a connection

(6.49)

\displaystyle\theta_{1}=\mathrm{d}z+m\,x\,\mathrm{d}y

on
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and an integral volume form

(6.50)

\displaystyle{\underline{H}}{}_{1}=k\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z\ ,

with
m,k\in\mathbb{Z}
, such that

(6.51)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{1}=k\qquad\text{and}\qquad\int_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\,c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})=m\ ,

where
c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{1})=\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}=m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y
represents the Chern class of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
.

On
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
we take as metric

(6.52)

\displaystyle{\underline{g}}{}_{1}=\pi_{1}^{*}\,g_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}+\theta_{1}\otimes\theta_{1}\ ,

where
g_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}
is the standard round metric on
{\mathsf{S}}^{2}
. Then
\frac{\partial}{\partial z},
a local expression of the generator of the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
-action, is a Killing vector field for
{\underline{g}}{}_{1}
.

Consider another three-dimensional
{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
-bundle
\pi_{2}\colon{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\to{\mathsf{S}}^{2}
endowed with connection

(6.53)

\displaystyle\theta_{2}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}+n\,x\,\mathrm{d}y

and Chern number
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
{\tilde{z}}
is the coordinate adapted to the fibres.
Let
M={\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
with projections
\varpi_{i}\colon M\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{i},
for
i=1,2,
and coordinates
20
20

20

We omit the pullback notation for the coordinates.

(x,y,z,\tilde{z})
pulled back from
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}.

In order to satisfy the conditions of Theorem
5.94
, we consider the two-form

(6.54)

\displaystyle B=-\varpi_{1}^{*}\theta_{1}\wedge\varpi_{2}^{*}\theta_{2}=x\,\mathrm{d}y\wedge(n\,\mathrm{d}z-m\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{z})-\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}

for our
B
-field transformation.

For
(\mathbb{T}M,H)
to be reducible with respect to
K_{2}={\,\rm e}\,^{-B}\mathfrak{k}_{2}
, we have that

(6.55)

\displaystyle\varpi_{1}^{*}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{1}-\mathrm{d}B

\displaystyle=k\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z+m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}-n\,\mathrm{d}z\wedge\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y

(6.56)

\displaystyle=(k-n)\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}z+m\,\mathrm{d}x\wedge\mathrm{d}y\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}

has to be basic with respect to the fibres of
\varpi_{2}
.
This happens if and only if
n=k
. Thus
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
has three-form
{\underline{H}}{}_{2}
and Chern form
c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})
satisfying

(6.57)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}\,{\underline{H}}{}_{2}=m\qquad\text{and}\qquad\int_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}\,c_{1}({\mathcal{Q}}_{2})=k\ ,

as well as metric obtained from Remark
6.26
as

(6.58)

\displaystyle{\underline{g}}{}_{2}=\pi_{2}^{*}\,g_{{\mathsf{S}}^{2}}+\theta_{2}\otimes\theta_{2}\ ,

which is again
{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
-invariant.

Hence the lens space
{\mathsf{L}}(m,k)
is T-dual to the lens space
{\mathsf{L}}(k,m)
, realising a topology change between T-dual spaces, see e.g.
[
17
]
. In particular, the Hopf fibration
{\mathsf{L}}(1,0)\simeq{\mathsf{S}}^{3}
without
H
-flux is T-dual to the trivial circle bundle
{\mathsf{L}}(0,1)\simeq{\mathsf{S}}^{2}\times{\mathsf{S}}^{1}
with
H
-flux. Moreover, the Hopf fibration with
H
-flux
{\mathsf{L}}(1,1)
is self-T-dual.

6.2.
Generalised T-duality for Para-Hermitian Manifolds

We shall now study the conditions under which the relation
R
becomes a generalised isometry when the manifolds
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
admit almost para-Hermitian structures, such that the diffeomorphism
\varphi
preserves their split-signature metrics.

6.2.1.
Para-Hermitian Manifolds

Let us start by reviewing the main properties of para-Hermitian manifolds, see
[
47
,
48
,
49
,
30
]
for more details.

Definition 6.59
.

An
almost para-complex structure
on an even-dimensional manifold
M
is an automorphism of the tangent bundle
TM
such that
{\mathscr{K}}^{2}=\mathds{1}_{TM}
and whose
\pm 1
-eigenbundles
L_{\pm}=\ker(\mathds{1}_{TM}\mp{\mathscr{K}})
have the same rank.

An
almost para-Hermitian structure
on
M
is a pair
(\eta,{{\mathscr{K}}})
of a split-signature metric
\eta
and an almost para-complex structure
{{\mathscr{K}}}\in\mathsf{Aut}(TM)
which is compatible with
\eta
in the sense that

(6.60)

\displaystyle\eta\big({{\mathscr{K}}}(X),{{\mathscr{K}}}(Y)\big)=-\eta(X,Y)

for all
X,Y\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(TM).
The triple
(M,\eta,{{\mathscr{K}}})
is an
almost para-Hermitian manifold
.

An almost para-Hermitian structure
(\eta,{{\mathscr{K}}})
is a
para-Hermitian structure
if
L_{\pm}
are integrable distributions, and in this case
(M,\eta,{{\mathscr{K}}})
is a
para-Hermitian manifold
.

We will often omit the adjective ‘almost’ for brevity, when no confusion can arise. Equation (
6.60
) implies that the eigenbundles
L_{\pm}
are maximally isotropic with respect to
\eta.
The splitting
TM=L_{+}\oplus L_{-}
defines a splitting

(6.61)

\displaystyle\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{p}T^{*}M=\bigoplus_{m+n=p}\,\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{+m,-n}T^{*}M\ ,

where
\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{+m,-n}T^{*}M=\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{m}L_{+}^{*}\otimes\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{n}L_{-}^{*}
.

A canonical Courant algebroid may be formed over an almost para-Hermitian manifold
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}})
in the following way.
21
21

21

See
[
50
]
for details of this construction and
[
51
,
52
,
48
,
30
,
53
]
for the definition of
{\tt D}
-bracket as well as the proof of this property and examples. See
[
54
, Section 4]
for the general definition of
\tt D
-brackets.
The Levi-Civita connection of the pseudo-Riemannian metric
\eta
defines a
\tt D
-bracket
[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]^{\texttt{LC}}
. The para-Hermitian structure
{\mathscr{K}}
defines a connection
\nabla^{\texttt{can}}
, and hence a canonical
\tt D
-bracket
[\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,]^{\texttt{can}}
.
We can then define a three-form
H^{\texttt{can}}
on
M
by

(6.62)

\displaystyle H^{\texttt{can}}(X,Y,Z)=\eta\big([X,Y]^{\texttt{LC}}-[X,Y]^{\texttt{can}},Z\big)\ ,

for
X,Y,Z\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(TM)
.

For our purposes, the
H
-flux is required to be basic with respect to
L_{-}
.
We thus take as our
H
-flux the
(+3,-0)
component of
H^{\texttt{can}}
. Assuming this is closed, we obtain a canonical Courant algebroid
\big(\mathbb{T}M,(H^{\texttt{can}})^{+3,-0}\big)
. We further have

(6.63)

\displaystyle H:=(H^{\texttt{can}})^{+3,-0}=\mathrm{d}\omega^{+3,-0}\ ,

where the almost symplectic structure
\omega:=\eta\circ{\mathscr{K}}
is called the
fundamental two-form
.

Remark 6.64

(
Wess-Zumino Functional and Integrability
)
.

The topological term of a sigma-model for a para-Hermitian manifold can be obtained from the lack of integrability of the eigenbundles of
{\mathscr{K}},
since

(6.65)

\displaystyle H(X_{-},Y_{-},Z_{-})=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\eta([X_{-},Y_{-}],Z_{-})-\eta([X_{-},Z_{-}],Y_{-})+\eta(X_{-},[Y_{-},Z_{-}])\bigr)\ ,

for all
X_{-},Y_{-},Z_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-})
. This vanishes if
L_{-}
is integrable. With
L_{-}=T{\mathcal{F}}_{-}
, a foliated almost para-Hermitian manifold
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}},{\mathcal{F}}_{-})
gives a topological term for the sigma-model if
H
is a closed three-form.
22
22

22

In other words, there exists a flat Ehresmann connection for the fibred manifold
M
over
{\mathcal{Q}}=M/{\mathcal{F}}_{-},
see
[
55
, Section 3.5]
.
From now on we assume this is the case.

We require a result describing generalised metrics on the tangent bundle
TM
of a para-Hermitian manifold
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}})
, rather than on the usual double tangent bundle
\mathbb{T}M
.
23
23

23

The latter is called the ‘large Courant algebroid’ in
[
56
,
57
]
, where the reductions to T-dual backgrounds are carried out in the language of three-dimensional (membrane) sigma-models.
To distinguish between these two notions of generalised metrics, we rename the former as

Definition 6.66
.

Let
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}})
be an almost para-Hermitian manifold. A
generalised para-Hermitian metric
is an automorphism
I\in\mathsf{Aut}(TM)
covering
\mathds{1}_{M}
such that
I^{2}=\mathds{1}_{TM}
and which, together with
\eta
, defines a Riemannian metric
{\mathcal{H}}
via

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}(X,Y)=\eta\big(I(X),Y\big)\ ,

for
X,Y\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(TM)
.

This is the counterpart of Definition
4.1
for tangent bundles of para-Hermitian manifolds. In this case we also have an equivalent formulation in terms of a subbundle
{\mathscr{V}}^{+}
of
TM
which is maximally positive-definite with respect to
\eta.
This is the
+1
-eigenbundle of
I
with its orthogonal complement with respect to
\eta
being the
-1
-eigenbundle.
The similarity extends even further, with the analogous results of Proposition
4.3
given by
[
30
]

Proposition 6.67
.

Let
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}})
be an almost para-Hermitian manifold, and
L_{\pm}\subset TM
the
\pm 1
-eigenbundles of
{\mathscr{K}}
.
A generalised para-Hermitian metric
I\in\mathsf{Aut}(TM)
defines a unique pair
(g_{+},b_{+})
given by a fibrewise Riemannian metric
g_{+}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
and a two-form
b_{+}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
.
Conversely, any such pair
(g_{+},b_{+})
defines a generalised para-Hermitian metric.

Remark 6.68

(
Characterisation of Generalised Para-Hermitian Metrics
)
.

Denoting by
\eta^{\flat}\in\mathsf{Hom}(TM,T^{*}M)
the musical isomorphism induced by
\eta,
one can define a metric
g_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}L_{-}^{*})
by

(6.69)

\displaystyle g_{-}(X_{-},Y_{-})=g_{+}^{-1}\big(\eta^{\flat}(X_{-}),\eta^{\flat}(Y_{-})\big)\ ,

for
X_{-},Y_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-}).
The two-form
b_{+}
defines a map
\gamma_{b}\colon L_{+}\to L_{-}
given by

\displaystyle b_{+}(X_{+},Y_{+})=\eta\big(\gamma_{b}(X_{+}),Y_{+}\big)\ ,

for
X_{+},Y_{+}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{+})
. The resulting Riemannian metric on
M
in the splitting
TM=L_{+}\oplus L_{-}
defined by
{\mathscr{K}}
is then

(6.70)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}=\left(\begin{matrix}g_{+}+\gamma_{b}^{\rm t}\,g_{-}\,\gamma_{b}&-\gamma^{\rm t}_{b}\,g_{-}\\
-g_{-}\,\gamma_{b}&g_{-}\end{matrix}\right)\ ,

where
\gamma_{b}^{\rm t}\colon L_{-}^{*}\to L_{+}^{*}
is the transpose map.

It follows that a generalised para-Hermitian metric is completely determined by the triple
(g_{+},b_{+},\eta).
Let us stress that the pair
(g_{+},b_{+})
is unique for the given splitting determined by the almost para-complex structure
{\mathscr{K}}.
A different pair would result in another splitting.

Equation (
6.70
) resembles Equation (
4.4
), though the background data in the latter are a Riemannian metric
g
and a two-form
b
on the tangent bundle
TM
, rather than on the subbundle
L_{+}
.

Remark 6.71

(
Bundle-like Generalised Para-Hermitian Metrics
)
.

Assume that
L_{-}
is involutive, thereby inducing a foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{-}
with smooth leaf space
{\mathcal{Q}}=M/{\mathcal{F}}_{-}.
Then the condition that
g_{+}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
and
b_{+}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
are pullbacks of a background metric and Kalb-Ramond field on
{\mathcal{Q}}
requires them to be basic:

\displaystyle\pounds_{X_{-}}g_{+}=0\qquad\text{and}\qquad\pounds_{X_{-}}b_{+}=0\ ,

for every
X_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-})
.
Notice that in order for
X_{-}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(L_{-})
to be a Killing vector field for
{\mathcal{H}},
it must be an infinitesimal symmetry of
\eta
as well:

(6.72)

\displaystyle\pounds_{X_{-}}\eta=0\ .

Conversely, starting with a Riemannian metric
{\underline{g}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}})
and a two-form
{\underline{b}}\in\mathsf{\Omega}^{2}({\mathcal{Q}})
, we can pull these back via the surjective submersion
\varpi\colon M\to{\mathcal{Q}}
, so that
\varpi^{*}{\underline{g}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\odot$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
and
\varpi^{*}{\underline{b}}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}L_{+}^{*})
, and hence define a generalised para-Hermitian metric
{\mathcal{H}}
via Equation (
6.70
).

The circumstances under which a diffeomorphism represents an isometry between generalised para-Hermitian metrics is provided by

Proposition 6.73
.

Let
(M_{1},\eta_{1},{\mathscr{K}}_{1})
and
(M_{2},\eta_{2},{\mathscr{K}}_{2})
be almost para-Hermitian manifolds endowed with generalised para-Hermitian metrics
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{2},
respectively. A diffeomorphism
\varphi\in\mathsf{Diff}(M_{1},M_{2})
is an isometry between
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
if and only if
\varphi
is an isometry between
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
which intertwines
I_{1}\in\mathsf{Aut}(TM_{1})
and
I_{2}\in\mathsf{Aut}(TM_{2})
:

(6.74)

\displaystyle\varphi_{*}\circ I_{1}=I_{2}\circ\varphi_{*}\ .

Proof.

Assume that
\varphi^{*}\eta_{2}=\eta_{1}
and Equation (
6.74
) hold. Then

(6.75)

\displaystyle\varphi^{*}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}(X,Y)

\displaystyle=\eta_{2}\big(\varphi_{*}(X),I_{2}(\varphi_{*}(Y))\big)=\eta_{2}\big(\varphi_{*}(X),\varphi_{*}(I_{1}(Y))\big)={\mathcal{H}}_{1}(X,Y)

for all
X,Y\in\mathsf{\Gamma}(TM_{1}),
where we use Equation (
6.74
) for the second equality and
\varphi^{*}\eta_{2}=\eta_{1}
for the third equality.
Conversely, assuming
\varphi^{*}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}={\mathcal{H}}_{1},
a similar argument establishes Equation (
6.74
) as well as that
\varphi
is an isometry between
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
.
∎

6.2.2.
The Reduced Courant Algebroid

Let
(M,\eta,{\mathscr{K}})
be an almost para-Hermitian manifold such that the
-1
-eigenbundle
L_{-}
is involutive, and the induced foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{-}
has smooth leaf space
{\mathcal{Q}}=M/{\mathcal{F}}_{-}
with smooth surjective submersion
\varpi\colon M\to{\mathcal{Q}}
. As discussed in Section
6.2.1
, there is a canonical Courant algebroid
(\mathbb{T}M,H)
, where
H=(H^{\texttt{can}})^{+3,-0}
is defined as in Equation (
6.62
) and assumed to be closed. Since
H
is basic with respect to
\varpi
, the reduction of Theorem
2.34
can be applied to give the Courant algebroid
(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}},{\underline{H}})
over
{\mathcal{Q}}
. The double tangent bundle
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}=T{\mathcal{Q}}\oplus T^{*}{\mathcal{Q}}
is pointwise isomorphic to
L_{+}\oplus L_{+}^{*}
.

6.2.3.
Generalised T-duality

We now describe
generalised T-duality
for para-Hermitian manifolds
[
30
]
in the language of Section
5
. This is done by first choosing diffeomorphic
2n
-dimensional manifolds
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
, and a diffeomorphism
\varphi\in\mathsf{Diff}(M_{1},M_{2})
.
Let
(\eta_{1},{\mathscr{K}}_{1})
be an almost para-Hermitian structure on
M_{1}
such that the
-1
-eigenbundle
L_{1-}
is involutive, and the induced foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
has smooth leaf space
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}=M_{1}/{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
with smooth surjective submersion
\varpi_{1}\colon M_{1}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
. Let
(M_{2},\eta_{2})
be a smooth manifold endowed with a split-signature metric
\eta_{2}
such that
\varphi^{*}\eta_{2}=\eta_{1}.
Choosing a T-duality direction in the quotient tangent space
T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
, an almost para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{2},{\mathscr{K}}_{2})
can be constructed on
M_{2}
which, provided its
-1
-eigenbundle
L_{2-}
is involutive and the induced foliation
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2-}
has smooth leaf space, gives the T-dual space (up to diffeomorphisms)
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{2}=M_{2}/{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2-}
to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
.

We discuss how this construction is obtained, starting with a local characterisation of the almost para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{1},{\mathscr{K}}_{1})
on
M_{1}.

Let
U_{1}\subset M_{1}
be an open subset associated with a coordinate chart, and choose a local frame
\set{Z_{I}}_{I=1,\dots,2n}=\set{Z_{i},\tilde{Z}^{i}}_{i=1,\dots,n}
which diagonalises
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
, i.e. such that
Z_{i}\in\mathsf{\Gamma}_{U_{1}}(L_{1+})
are
+1
-eigenvectors and
\tilde{Z}^{i}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{U_{1}}(L_{1-})
are
-1
-eigenvectors of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
at every point in
U_{1}.
Denote the dual coframe by
\set{\Theta^{I}}_{I=1,\dots,2n}=

\set{\Theta^{i},\tilde{\Theta}_{i}}_{i=1,\dots,n}
. In this frame, the tensors
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}|_{U_{1}}
,
\eta|_{U_{1}}
and
\omega_{1}|_{U_{1}}
can be written as
24
24

24

We use the Einstein convention for summation over repeated upper and lower indices throughout.

(6.76)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}_{1}|_{U_{1}}=\Theta^{i}\otimes Z_{i}-\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\otimes\tilde{Z}^{i}\quad,\quad\eta_{1}|_{U_{1}}=\Theta^{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{i}+\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\otimes\Theta^{i}\quad,\quad\omega_{1}|_{U_{1}}=\Theta^{i}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\ .

In the chart
U_{1}
, the frame fields

\set{Z_{I}}_{I=1,\dots,2n}
close a Lie algebra

[Z_{I},Z_{J}]=C_{IJ}{}^{K}\,Z_{K}\ .

In the splitting
TM_{1}=L_{1+}\oplus L_{1-}
this
becomes the Lie algebra

(6.77)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{[Z_{i},Z_{j}]=f_{ij}{}^{k}\,Z_{k}+H_{ijk}\,\tilde{Z}^{k}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
&{[Z_{i},\tilde{Z}^{j}]=f_{ki}{}^{j}\,\tilde{Z}^{k}+Q_{i}{}^{jk}\,Z_{k}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
&{[\tilde{Z}^{i},\tilde{Z}^{j}]=Q_{k}{}^{ij}\,\tilde{Z}^{k}+R^{ijk}\,Z_{k}}\ .\end{aligned}

That
L_{1-}
is integrable is equivalent to
R^{ijk}=0
for all
i,j,k=1,\dots,n
. Lemma
6.91
below tells us that
Z_{i}
are
\varpi_{1}
-projectable if and only if
Q_{i}{}^{jk}=0
.

Remark 6.78
.

In string theory the local structure functions in Equation
6.77
are called
generalised fluxes
. In particular,
f_{ij}{}^{k}
and
H_{ijk}
are known as
geometric fluxes
, while
Q_{i}{}^{jk}
and
R^{ijk}
are
non-geometric fluxes
. Only when
R^{ijk}
vanishes is a reduction to a smooth quotient space
{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}=M_{1}/{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
possible.

The condition that
Q_{i}{}^{jk}
vanishes tells us that
L_{1-}
is locally abelian. Note that the chart diagonalising
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
need not be a chart adapted to the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
, in which we could write
\tilde{Z}^{i}=\tfrac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}_{i}}
where
\tilde{x}_{i}
are coordinates adapted to the leaves. Conversely, if the diagonalising chart is adapted to the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
, then
Q_{i}{}^{jk}
vanish. That is, even though
L_{1-}
is always locally abelian (by looking in an adapted chart), we require that the (possibly not adapted) chart diagonalising
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
is also locally abelian.

Note also that for the procedure to work, we do not require that the local diagonalising frame fields
Z_{i}
and dual one-forms
\Theta^{i}
are basic sections, since the only requirement for reduction of an
H_{1}
-twisted standard Courant algebroid is that
H_{1}
is basic, see Example
2.60
. Thus the condition that
Q_{i}{}^{jk}
vanish could be relaxed, though in that case the resulting relations
Q(L_{i-})
and
R
would have a more complex and less insightful description. Thus, for sake of simplicity, we keep this assumption.

Assuming
Q_{i}{}^{jk}=0
, the vector fields
\underline{Z}{}_{i}\coloneqq\varpi_{1*}(Z_{i})
give local coordinates for the chart
\underline{U}{}_{1}\coloneqq\varpi_{1}(U_{1})\subset{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
. Fixing
d\in\set{1,\dots,n}
, define
D_{1}|_{\underline{U}{}_{1}}={\rm Span}\set{\underline{Z}{}_{1},\dots,\underline{Z}{}_{d}}
. This can be done in a neighbourhood of every point in
M_{1}
, and so gives a subbundle
D_{1}\subset T{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
which will become the T-duality directions, as in Definition
5.29
.
On
M_{1}
this gives the local frame for
U_{1}
as
25
25

25

From now on, upper case Latin indices run from
1
to
2n
, lower case Latin indices run from
1
to
n
, lower case Greek letters run from
d+1
to
n
, and mirrored lower case Greek letters run from
1
to
d
.

(6.79)

\displaystyle\set{Z_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}},Z_{\mu},\tilde{Z}^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}},\tilde{Z}^{\nu}}\ ,\quad\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}\in\set{1,\dots,d}\ ,\ \mu,\nu\in\set{d+1,\dots,n}\ .

To obtain an almost para-Hermitian structure on
M_{2}
, notice that
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\eta_{1}
defines a split-signature metric on
M_{2}
. In the coordinate chart
U_{2}\coloneqq\varphi(U_{1})
, define the vectors

(6.80)

\displaystyle Z_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}^{\prime}\coloneqq\varphi_{*}(\tilde{Z}^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}})\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}\coloneqq\varphi_{*}(Z_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}})\quad,\quad Z_{\mu}^{\prime}\coloneqq\varphi_{*}(Z_{\mu})\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime\nu}\coloneqq\varphi_{*}(\tilde{Z}^{\nu})\ ,

which give the respective dual one-forms
\Theta^{\prime\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}
,
\Theta^{\prime\mu}
,
\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}
and
\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{\nu}
whose pullbacks satisfy similar equations to those of Equation (
6.80
). The almost para-Hermitian structure on
U_{2}
is then given by

(6.81)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}_{2}|_{U_{2}}=\Theta^{\prime i}\otimes Z^{\prime}_{i}-\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\tilde{Z}^{\prime i}\quad,\quad\eta_{2}|_{U_{2}}=\Theta^{\prime i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}+\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\Theta^{\prime i}\quad,\quad\omega_{2}|_{U_{2}}=\Theta^{\prime i}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\ .

Since we can make this construction in the neighbourhood of every point, we get an almost para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{2},{\mathscr{K}}_{2})
on
M_{2}
for which
\set{Z^{\prime}_{I}}_{I=1,\dots,2n}=\set{Z^{\prime}_{i},\tilde{Z}^{\prime i}}_{i=1,\dots,n}
is a diagonalising frame for
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
in the coordinate chart
U_{2}
.

We thus see that
\varphi^{*}\eta_{2}=\eta_{1}
, while
\varphi^{*}{\mathscr{K}}_{2}\neq{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
. The pullback
\varphi^{*}{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
defines an almost para-complex structure
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}^{\prime}
on
M_{1}
compatible with
\eta_{1}
, and hence gives a new splitting
TM_{1}=L_{1+}^{\prime}\oplus L_{1-}^{\prime}
. On the coordinate chart
U_{1}
, define

(6.82)

\displaystyle L_{1}^{++}|_{U_{1}}=L_{1+}|_{U_{1}}\cap L_{1+}^{\prime}|_{U_{1}}\quad,\quad L_{1}^{+-}|_{U_{1}}=L_{1+}|_{U_{1}}\cap L_{1-}^{\prime}|_{U_{1}}\ ,

(6.83)

\displaystyle L_{1}^{-+}|_{U_{1}}=L_{1-}|_{U_{1}}\cap L_{1+}^{\prime}|_{U_{1}}\quad,\quad L_{1}^{--}|_{U_{1}}=L_{1-}|_{U_{1}}\cap L_{1-}^{\prime}|_{U_{1}}\ .

Notice that
L_{1}^{++}|_{U_{1}}={\rm Span}\set{Z_{\mu}}_{\mu=d+1,\dots,n}
, and hence it patches into a subbundle
L_{1}^{++}
. Similarly
L_{1}^{+-}
,
L_{1}^{-+}
and
L_{1}^{--}
are subbundles of
TM_{1}
. In particular,
L_{1}^{--}
has constant rank.

Lemma
6.91
below gives the conditions on the local structure functions for the chosen frame such that
L_{2-}
, the
-1
-eigenbundle of
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
, is integrable and hence defines a regular foliation
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{2-}
. Assuming this holds, and assuming the quotient
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\coloneqq M_{1}/{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}=\varphi^{-1}({\mathcal{F}}_{2}^{\prime})
, is smooth with smooth surjective submersion
\varpi_{2}\colon M_{1}\to{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
, we obtain the candidate T-dual manifold
{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. We set
C\coloneqq\set{(\varpi_{1}(m_{1}),\varpi_{2}(m_{1}))\,}{\,m_{1}\in M_{1}}
. As discussed in Section
5.1
, this will be the support of our T-duality relation. Theorem
5.9
requires that
C
is smooth, which we assume, and that
L_{1}^{--}
has constant rank, which we have already established.

To obtain a T-duality relation
R\colon\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\dashrightarrow\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
supported on
C
, in light of Example
5.26
we construct a Courant algebroid isomorphism
\Phi\coloneqq\overline{\varphi}\circ{\,\rm e}\,^{B}\colon\mathbb{T}M_{1}\to\mathbb{T}M_{2}
covering
\varphi
. Thus we need an appropriate
B
-field. In para-Hermitian geometry, a
B
-field transformation from
L_{1+}
to
L_{1-}
is induced by a two-form
B_{+}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{2}L_{1+}^{*})
. The fundamental two-form
\omega_{1}
and the canonical three-form
H_{1}^{\texttt{can}}
map to

(6.84)

\displaystyle\omega_{B_{+}}=\omega_{1}-2\,B_{+}\qquad\text{and}\qquad H_{B_{+}}^{\texttt{can}}=H_{1}^{\texttt{can}}-\mathrm{d}B_{+}

by Equation (
6.62
) and
[
54
, Proposition 5.9]
, while the split-signature metric
\eta_{1}
is preserved.

Following this, we use the
\eta_{1}
-compatible para-complex structures
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
and
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}^{\prime}\coloneqq\varphi^{*}{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
on
M_{1}
, with respective fundamental two-forms
\omega_{1}
and
\omega_{1}^{\prime}\coloneqq\varphi^{*}\omega_{2}
, to define

(6.85)

\displaystyle B=\tfrac{1}{2}\,(\omega_{1}-\omega_{1}^{\prime})\ .

Locally this is given by

(6.86)

\displaystyle B|_{U_{1}}=\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\wedge{\tilde{\Theta}}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\ .

The construction of T-duality relation
R
realised through

Proposition 6.87
.

Suppose that the structure functions for the local Lie algebra (
6.77
) satisfy

(6.88)

\displaystyle R^{ijk}=0\ ,\ Q_{i}{}^{jk}=0\ ,\ f_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip for every

,
\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}=1,\dots,d
and
\mu=d+1,\dots,n
.
Suppose moreover that
H_{1}
is closed.
Then the T-duality relation

(6.89)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2})}

exists, where, in the setting of Example
5.26
,
\Phi\coloneqq\overline{\varphi}\circ{\,\rm e}\,^{B}\,
with
B=\frac{1}{2}\,(\omega_{1}-\varphi^{*}\omega_{2})
and
\overline{\varphi}=\varphi_{*}+(\varphi^{-1})^{*}
, while
H_{2}
is locally given by

(6.90)

\displaystyle H_{2}|_{\varphi(U_{1})}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,f_{\mu\nu}{}^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\,\delta_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}\,\Theta^{\prime\mu}\wedge\Theta^{\prime\nu}\wedge\Theta^{\prime\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\,H_{\mu\nu\alpha}\,\Theta^{\prime\mu}\wedge\Theta^{\prime\nu}\wedge\Theta^{\prime\alpha}\ .

Before constructing the Courant algebroid relation
R
, we describe the conditions on the structure functions through

Lemma 6.91
.

Let
U_{1}\subset M_{1}
be any coordinate chart.

1)

The
-1
-eigenbundle of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
is integrable if and only if the structure functions of Equation (
6.77
) satisfy

(6.92)

\displaystyle R^{ijk}=0\ ,

for
i,j,k=1,\dots,n
.

2)

If Equation (
6.92
) holds, the diagonalising local sections
Z_{i}
for
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
with
i=1,\dots,n
are projectable with respect to
\varpi_{1}
if and only if

(6.93)

\displaystyle Q_{i}{}^{jk}=0\ ,

for
i,j,k=1,\dots,n
.
This also ensures that
\Theta^{i}
is basic with respect to
\varpi_{1}
for
i=1,\dots,n
, and that the bisubmersion condition (
5.22
) is satisfied.

6.92
) and (

for all
\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}=1,\dots,d
and
\mu,\nu=d+1,\dots,n
.

If Equations (

)–(

) hold and the three-form
