for all
\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\alpha$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}=1,\dots,d
and
\mu,\nu=d+1,\dots,n
.

Proof.

We have already mentioned the first item. The projectability of the local sections
Z_{i}
and the bisubmersion condition from item
2)
, as well as item
3)
, can be read off immediately from Equation (
6.77
). For example, in a local coordinate chart
U_{1}
, the vector fields
Z_{i}
are projectable with respect to
\varpi_{1}
if and only if
[X,Z_{i}]\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{U_{1}}(L_{1-})
for every
X\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{U_{1}}(L_{1-})
, which happens if and only if
[{\tilde{Z}}^{j},Z_{i}]\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}_{U_{1}}(L_{1-})
for every
i,j=1,\dots,n
, hence
Q_{i}{}^{jk}=0
.

To show that
\Theta^{i}
are basic, we consider the Maurer-Cartan equations for the coframe
\set{\Theta^{I}}_{I=1,\dots,2n}
:

(6.96)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\Theta^{I}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,C_{JL}{}^{I}\,\Theta^{J}\wedge\Theta^{L}\ .

In the splitting
TM_{1}=L_{1+}\oplus L_{1-}
this reads

(6.97)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathrm{d}\Theta^{i}&=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big(f_{jk}{}^{i}\,\Theta^{j}\wedge\Theta^{k}+R^{ijk}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{j}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{k}\big)-Q_{k}{}^{ji}\,\Theta^{k}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{j}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\mathrm{d}\tilde{\Theta}_{i}&=f_{ij}{}^{k}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{k}\wedge\Theta^{j}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\big(Q_{i}{}^{jk}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{j}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{k}+H_{ijk}\,\Theta^{j}\wedge\Theta^{k}\big)\ .\end{aligned}

The one-form
\Theta^{i}
is basic with respect to
\varpi_{1}
, for
i=1,\dots,n
, if and only if
\iota_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\Theta^{i}=0
and
\pounds_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\Theta^{i}=0
for each
j=1,\dots,n
. The first condition holds by definition, thus assuming Equations (
6.92
) and (
6.93
) we get

(6.98)

\displaystyle\pounds_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\Theta^{i}=\iota_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\,\mathrm{d}\Theta^{i}+\mathrm{d}\,\iota_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\Theta^{i}=-\tfrac{1}{2}\,f_{kl}{}^{i}\,\iota_{{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}\,\big(\Theta^{k}\wedge\Theta^{l}\big)=0\ .

To show item
4)
, recall that
B=\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\wedge{\tilde{\Theta}}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}
, and since
H_{1}=\mathrm{d}\omega_{1}^{+3,-0}
one must find the constraints such that
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(H_{1}-\mathrm{d}B)
is basic, i.e.
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(H_{1}-\mathrm{d}B)\in{{\mathsf{\Gamma}}}(\text{\Large$\wedge$}^{+3,-0}T^{*}M_{2}).

After a lengthy calculation, one arrives at the conditions (
4)
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.87
.

Let
K_{1}=L_{1-}\oplus\set{0}
and
K_{2}=\Phi^{-1}(L_{2-}\oplus\set{0})
. Since the three-form
H_{1}=\mathrm{d}\omega_{1}^{+3,-0}
is closed, the assumption
\iota_{X}H_{1}=0,
for all
X\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(L_{1})
of Proposition
5.27
is satisfied.

The condition
\iota_{X}H_{2}=0,
for all
X\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}(L_{2})
of Proposition
5.27
is satisfied under the assumption of Equation (
6.88
), and since
H_{1}
is closed it follows that
H_{2}\coloneqq(\varphi^{-1})^{*}(H_{1}-\mathrm{d}B)
is closed. Hence the Courant algebroids
(\mathbb{T}M_{1},H_{1})
and
(\mathbb{T}M_{2},H_{2})
are isomorphic via
\Phi
.

Finally,
\ker(B|_{T{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}\cap(T{\mathcal{F}}_{2-})})=T{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}\cap T{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}=L_{1}^{--}
has constant rank
n-d
, and hence all the assumptions of Proposition
5.27
are satisfied.

Applying Proposition
5.27
we thus obtain a Courant algebroid relation supported on
C=\set{(q_{1},q_{2})\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}\,}{\,q_{1}=\varpi_{1}(m_{1})\ ,\ q_{2}=\varpi_{2}(m_{1})\ ,\ m_{1}\in M_{1}}
which, at a point
(q_{1},q_{2})\in C
, is given by

(6.99)

\displaystyle R_{(q_{1},q_{2})}=\text{Span}\set{({\underline{Z}}{}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}},{\underline{\Theta}}^{\prime\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}})\,,\,({\underline{Z}}{}_{\mu},{\underline{Z}}{}^{\prime}_{\mu})\,,\,({\underline{\Theta}}^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}},{\underline{Z}}^{\prime}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}})\,,\,({\underline{\Theta}}^{\nu},{\underline{\Theta}}^{\prime\nu})}\ \subset\ \mathbb{T}_{q_{1}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}\times\overline{\mathbb{T}_{q_{2}}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}}\ ,

as required.
∎

From now on, we assume that the conditions of Proposition
6.87
are satisfied. Having constructed the T-duality relation
R
, we show that it gives a generalised isometry when appropriate background fields are taken on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
and
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
. The first step is to provide conditions under which the
B
-field has the required symmetry, which we establish through

Lemma 6.100
.

\pounds_{X}B=0
for each
X\in\set{Z_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}},{\tilde{Z}}^{\mu}}^{\mu=d+1,\dots,n}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}=1,\dots,d}
if and only if
H_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}\mu}=H_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\mu\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}
for each
\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$},\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}=1,\dots,d
and
\mu=d+1,\dots,n
.

Proof.

The proof again follows from the Maurer-Cartan equations (
6.97
) and Cartan calculus.
∎

Assuming the conditions of Lemma
6.100
from now on, we then find

Corollary 6.101
.

In a coordinate chart
U_{1}
, the three-form
H_{1}
is given by

(6.102)

\displaystyle H_{1}|_{U_{1}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\,H_{i\mu\nu}\,\Theta^{i}\wedge\Theta^{\mu}\wedge\Theta^{\nu}\ .

Equation (
6.102
) is the analog of
[
8
, Equation (2.5)]
.

Take a generalised metric
V_{1}^{+}
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
specified by
({\underline{g}}{}_{1},{\underline{b}}{}_{1})
. Locally, we suppose that

(6.103)

\displaystyle\underline{g}_{1}=\underline{g}_{ij}\underline{\Theta}^{i}\otimes\underline{\Theta}^{j}\,,\quad\underline{b}_{1}=\underline{b}_{ij}\underline{\Theta}^{i}\wedge\underline{\Theta}^{j}\,\,\text{ such that }\quad\pounds_{\underline{Z}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}}\underline{g}_{ij}=\pounds_{\underline{Z}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}}\underline{b}_{ij}=0.

Hence we define
\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})\coloneqq\mathrm{Span}_{\mathbb{R}}\set{Z_{1},\dots,Z_{d}}
, so that
V_{1}^{+}
is
D_{1}
-invariant. The two-form
B
decomposes as in Equation (
5.87
) (with
B_{\rm hor}=B_{\rm ver}=0
), and by Lemma
6.100
, the conditions of Theorem
5.94
are satisfied. Thus

Corollary 6.104
.

With
V_{1}^{+}
given by (
6.103
), there is a unique background
V_{2}^{+}
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
given by
({\underline{g}}{}_{2},{\underline{b}}{}_{2})
such that
R
is a generalised isometry between
V_{1}^{+}
and
V_{2}^{+}
.

While there is always a way to construct the unique generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
, with the extra structure a para-Hermitian manifold carries there is cleaner way to find it.

Remarks
6.68
and
6.71
show that we get a generalised para-Hermitian metric
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
on
TM_{1}
. Similarly, the T-dual background
({\underline{g}}{}_{2},{\underline{b}}{}_{2})
on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2}
gives a generalised para-Hermitian metric
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
on
TM_{2}
. Since
\varphi
is an isometry between the split-signature metrics
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2},
we can show that it is also an isometry between
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}
through

Proposition 6.105
.

Let
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1},\underline{H}{}_{1},({\underline{g}}{}_{1},{\underline{b}}{}_{1}))\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{2},\underline{H}{}_{2},({\underline{g}}{}_{2},{\underline{b}}{}_{2}))
be the generalised isometry coming from Theorem
5.46
applied to the
\eta
-isometric almost para-Hermitian manifolds
(M_{1},\eta_{1},{\mathscr{K}}_{1})
and
(M_{2},\eta_{2},{\mathscr{K}}_{2})
. Then
\varphi^{*}{\mathcal{H}}_{2}={\mathcal{H}}_{1}
.

Proof.

Assume, by absorbing the two-form
{\underline{b}}{}_{1}
into
{\underline{H}{}_{1}}
if necessary, that the background on
\mathbb{T}{\mathcal{Q}}_{1}
is given solely by the Riemannian metric
{\underline{g}}{}_{1}
. Using Equation (
6.70
), the generalised para-Hermitian metric
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}
thus takes the form

(6.106)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}g_{1+}&0\\
0&g_{1-}\end{pmatrix}\ .

The four-fold splitting

TM_{1}=L_{1}^{+-}\oplus L_{1}^{++}\oplus L_{1}^{--}\oplus L_{1}^{-+}

gives a decomposition of
g_{1+}
into four parts:

(6.107)

\displaystyle g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}(L_{1}^{+-})^{*}\big)\quad

\displaystyle,\quad g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big((L_{1}^{+-})^{*}\otimes(L_{1}^{++})^{*}\big)\ ,

(6.108)

\displaystyle g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big((L_{1}^{++})^{*}\otimes(L_{1}^{+-})^{*}\big)\quad

\displaystyle,\quad g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}(L_{1}^{++})^{*}\big)\ ,

where
g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}=\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)^{\rm t}=g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}
.
Locally this decomposition is given explicitly by

(6.109)

\displaystyle g_{1+}|_{U_{1}}=(g_{1+})_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}\,\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\otimes\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}+(g_{1+})_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\mu}\,\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\otimes\Theta^{\mu}+(g_{1+})_{\mu\nu}\,\Theta^{\mu}\otimes\Theta^{\nu}\ ,

with
(g_{1+})_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}=\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}{\mskip 2.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\nu$\hss}\mskip 8.0mu}$}}
,
(g_{1+})_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\mu}=\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\big)_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}\mu}
and
(g_{1+})_{\mu\nu}=\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\big)_{\mu\nu}
.

The T-dual generalised metric
V_{2}^{+}
is determined by the reduction of the basic tensors
g_{2+}\in{\mathsf{\Gamma}}\big(\text{\Large$\otimes$}^{2}(L_{2+})^{*}\big)
, with components
g_{2}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}
,

and

, and

, with components

,

and

where

Since
B=\Theta^{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{\text{$\mathchoice{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\displaystyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\textstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}{\mskip 4.0mu{-10.51}\hbox to0.0pt{\hss$\scriptscriptstyle\mu$\hss}\mskip 7.0mu}$}}
, by a calculation similar to that of Remark

this can be written locally as

This comes from the formula for the inverse of a

matrix.

Applying
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}
to Equation (
6.113
) yields

(6.117)

\displaystyle(\varphi^{-1})^{*}{\mathcal{H}}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}&0&0&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\\[3.0pt]
0&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}&0\\[3.0pt]
0&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}&0\\[3.0pt]
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}&0&0&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

This is in the form of Equation (
6.70
), and hence
we can read off the metric
g_{2+}
and two-form
b_{2+}
on
L_{2+}
as

(6.118)

\displaystyle g_{2+}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)^{-1}&0\\[3.0pt]
0&(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\ ,

(6.119)

\displaystyle b_{2+}

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}0&-(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)^{-1}\,g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\\[3.0pt]
(\varphi^{-1})^{*}g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}}\,\big(g_{1}^{\mathrel{\mathop{+}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle+$}\vss}}}\mathrel{\mathop{-}\limits^{\vbox to0.0pt{\kern-2.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}\vss}}}}\big)^{-1}&0\end{pmatrix}\ .

Looking in a coordinate chart
\varphi(U_{1})=U_{2}\subset M_{2}
, we find that this agrees with Equation (
6.112
).
∎

Equation (
6.112
) gives the component form of the Buscher rules for generalised T-duality in para-Hermitian geometry.

6.3.
Doubled Nilmanifolds

In this final section we consider the class of string theory compactifications provided by nilmanifolds, which are quotients of nilpotent Lie groups by a discrete cocompact subgroup.
We focus on the particular example of the three-dimensional Heisenberg nilmanifold
\mathsf{N}(m,k)
, which is a quotient of the Heisenberg group
\mathsf{H}
of upper triangular
3\times 3
matrices whose diagonal entries are all equal to
1
; geometrically it defines a circle bundle of degree
m\in\mathbb{Z}
over a two-torus
{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
with
H
-flux representing the Ševera class labelled by
k\in\mathbb{Z}.
This is analogous to the lens space
\mathsf{L}(m,k)
from Example
6.48
, which is a quotient of
{\mathsf{S}}^{3}\simeq\mathsf{SU}(2)
by a cyclic subgroup
\mathbb{Z}_{m}
of the isometry group
\mathsf{SU}(2)
of the round three-sphere. In particular, it can be similarly treated in the correspondence space picture of Section
6.1
, giving a topology changing T-duality between
\mathsf{N}(m,k)
and
\mathsf{N}(k,m)
.

Here we consider this T-duality relation instead in the framework of Section
6.2
by compactifying the Drinfel’d double
T^{*}\mathsf{H}={\mathsf{H}}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
, the cotangent bundle of the Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}
, by the action of a discrete cocompact subgroup which defines a doubled nilmanifold
[
31
,
32
,
58
]
. We illustrate this explicitly for the simplest case with
k=0
, where
\mathsf{N}(m,0)
is the nilmanifold
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
of degree
m
without
H
-flux and
\mathsf{N}(0,m)
is the three-torus
\mathsf{T}^{3}
with Ševera class
m
. We shall study two foliations of the doubled Heisenberg nilmanifold, and obtain a T-duality between the respective quotients in sense of Section
6.2
.

6.3.1.
The Drinfel’d Double of the Heisenberg Group

The doubled Heisenberg nilmanifold is constructed by considering a quotient of the Drinfel’d double
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
of the three-dimensional Heisenberg group
{\mathsf{H}}
given by
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}=T^{*}\mathsf{H}=\mathsf{H}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3},
with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{h}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
, by a discrete cocompact subgroup
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
.
Here the Heisenberg algebra
\mathfrak{h}={\sf Lie}({\mathsf{H}})
and the abelian Lie algebra
\mathbb{R}^{3}
together with
\mathfrak{d}=\mathfrak{h}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
form a Manin triple.
Despite the fact that
{\mathsf{H}}
is not semi-simple, we can still give a matrix representation for the Lie algebra of the Drinfel’d double
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}
. This is useful for explicitly writing down the coordinate identifications defining the global structure of the doubled nilmanifold.

In local coordinates
(x,y,z,\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})\in{\mathsf{H}}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, an element
\gamma
in
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
may be written as

\displaystyle\gamma=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}1&m\,x&y&0&0&\tilde{z}\\
0&1&z&0&0&-\tilde{y}\\
0&0&1&0&0&0\\
0&-m\,\tilde{y}&\tilde{x}-m\,z\,\tilde{y}&1&m\,x&y+\frac{1}{2}\,m\,\tilde{y}^{2}\\
0&0&0&0&1&z\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{array}\right)\ .

Then the left-invariant one-forms on
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
are the Lie algebra components of
the corresponding Maurer-Cartan one-form
\Theta=\gamma^{-1}\,\mathrm{d}\gamma
given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Theta^{x}=\mathrm{d}x\quad,\quad\Theta^{y}=\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z\quad,\quad\Theta^{z}=\mathrm{d}z\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{\Theta}_{x}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}-m\,z\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\quad,\quad\tilde{\Theta}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\quad,\quad\tilde{\Theta}_{z}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}+m\,x\,\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}\ .\end{gathered}

The dual left-invariant vector fields are therefore

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}Z_{x}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\quad,\quad Z_{y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\quad,\quad Z_{z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial z}+m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial y}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{Z}^{x}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}}+m\,z\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}}-m\,x\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}}\ .\end{gathered}

The nilpotent Lie algebra
\mathfrak{d}
of
T^{*}{\mathsf{H}}={\mathsf{H}}\ltimes\mathbb{R}^{3}
thus has non-vanishing brackets

(6.124)

\displaystyle[Z_{x},Z_{z}]=m\,Z_{y}\ ,\quad[Z_{x},\tilde{Z}^{y}]=-m\,\tilde{Z}^{z}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad[Z_{z},\tilde{Z}^{y}]=m\,\tilde{Z}^{x}\ ,

and in particular the only non-vanishing structure function is
f_{xz}{}^{y}=-f_{zx}{}^{y}=m.

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{d}
is naturally endowed with a para-Hermitian structure which induces a left-invariant para-Hermitian structure
(\eta^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},{\mathscr{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1})
on
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
given by

(6.125)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}=\Theta^{i}\otimes Z_{i}-{\tilde{\Theta}}_{i}\otimes{\tilde{Z}}^{i}\quad,\quad\eta^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}=\Theta^{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{i}+\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\otimes\Theta^{i}\quad,\quad\omega^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}=\Theta^{i}\wedge{\tilde{\Theta}}_{i}\ .

This makes
({\mathsf{D}}^{{\mathsf{H}}},\eta^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1},{\mathscr{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1})
a para-Hermitian manifold.
The eigenbundles of
{\mathscr{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1}
are then the integrable distributions given by
L^{\mathfrak{d}}_{+}
spanned by the left-invariant vector fields
\set{Z_{x},Z_{y},Z_{z}}
induced by the Lie subalgebra
\mathfrak{h}
whose leaves are all given by the Heisenberg group
\mathsf{H}
, and
L^{\mathfrak{d}}_{-}
spanned by
\set{\tilde{Z}^{x},\tilde{Z}^{y},\tilde{Z}^{z}}
coming from the generators of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
whose leaves are just
\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
The global frame
\set{Z_{i},{\tilde{Z}}_{j}}
for
i,j\in\set{x,y,z}
defines a diagonalising frame for the para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{1}^{\mathfrak{d}},{\mathscr{K}}^{\mathfrak{d}}_{1})
.

6.3.2.
The Doubled Heisenberg Nilmanifold

To compactify
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
, we consider the left action by a discrete cocompact subgroup
{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})
whose generic element
\xi
is given by

\displaystyle\xi=\left(\begin{array}[]{cccccc}1&m\,\alpha&\beta&0&0&\tilde{\delta}\\
0&1&\delta&0&0&-\tilde{\beta}\\
0&0&1&0&0&0\\
0&-m\,\tilde{\beta}&\tilde{\alpha}-m\,\delta\,\tilde{\beta}&1&m\alpha&\beta+\frac{1}{2}\,m\,\tilde{\beta}^{2}\\
0&0&0&0&1&\delta\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{array}\right)\ ,

where
\alpha,\beta,\delta,\tilde{\alpha},\tilde{\beta},\tilde{\delta}\in\mathbb{Z}
. Under the identification
\gamma\sim\xi\,\gamma
, we get the simultaneous identifications of coordinates as

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}x\sim x+\alpha\quad,\quad y\sim y+m\,\alpha\,z+\beta\quad,\quad z\sim z+\delta\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{x}\sim\tilde{x}+m\,\delta\,\tilde{y}+\tilde{\alpha}\quad,\quad\tilde{y}\sim\tilde{y}+\tilde{\beta}\quad,\quad\tilde{z}\sim\tilde{z}-m\,\alpha\,\tilde{y}+\tilde{\delta}\ ,\end{gathered}

which defines the doubled Heisenberg nilmanifold
M^{\mathsf{H}}={\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus{\mathsf{D}}^{\mathsf{H}}
.

The left-invariant one-forms (
6.3.1
) and left-invariant vector fields (
6.3.1
) are invariant under the identifications (
6.3.2
), and hence descend globally through the quotient. Thus the distributions
L^{\mathfrak{d}}_{+}
and
L^{\mathfrak{d}}_{-}
descend respectively to integrable distributions
L_{1\pm}\subset TM^{\mathsf{H}}
. Their leaves are, respectively, the Heisenberg nilmanifold
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}=\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Z})\setminus\mathsf{H}
and the three-torus
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}=\mathbb{R}^{3}/\mathbb{Z}^{3}
.

The para-Hermitian structure (
6.125
) is left-invariant, hence invariant under the left discrete action. Thus it descends to a para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{1},{\mathscr{K}}_{1})
on
M^{\mathsf{H}}
, with integrable

\pm 1
-eigenbundles
L_{1\pm}
and globally defined diagonalising frame
\set{Z_{i},{\tilde{Z}}^{j}}
for
i,j\in\set{x,y,z}
.
Since
L_{1\pm}
are involutive, Remark
6.64
tells us that
H_{1}=0
.

6.3.3.
Generalised T-duality

Consider the diffeomorphism of
M_{1}:=M^{\mathsf{H}}
given in coordinates by

(6.128)

\displaystyle\varphi(x,y,z,\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{z})=(x,\tilde{y},z,\tilde{x}-m\,z\,\tilde{y},y,\tilde{z})\eqqcolon(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime},\tilde{x}^{\prime},\tilde{y}^{\prime},\tilde{z}^{\prime})\ ,

with the coordinate identifications

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}x^{\prime}\sim x^{\prime}+\alpha^{\prime}\quad,\quad y^{\prime}\sim y^{\prime}+\tilde{\beta}^{\prime}\quad,\quad z^{\prime}\sim z^{\prime}+\delta^{\prime}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{x}^{\prime}\sim\tilde{x}^{\prime}+m\,\delta^{\prime}\,\tilde{y}^{\prime}+m\,z^{\prime}\,(m\,\alpha^{\prime}\,z^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime})+\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime}\quad,\quad\tilde{y}^{\prime}\sim\tilde{y}^{\prime}+m\,\alpha^{\prime}\,z^{\prime}+\beta^{\prime}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{z}^{\prime}\sim\tilde{z}^{\prime}-m\,\alpha^{\prime}\,y^{\prime}+\tilde{\delta}^{\prime}\ ,\end{gathered}

where
\alpha^{\prime},\beta^{\prime},\delta^{\prime},\tilde{\alpha}^{\prime},\tilde{\beta}^{\prime},\tilde{\delta}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Since the only non-vanishing structure function is
f_{xz}{}^{y}
, by Proposition
6.87
it follows that the only T-duality direction we can consider is in the
y
-direction, that is,
D_{1}=\text{Span}\set{\underline{Z}{}_{y}}
.
The global frame fields on
M_{2}:=\varphi(M^{\mathsf{H}})
, where the T-duality direction is swapped as in Equation (
6.80
), are given by

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}Z_{x}^{\prime}=\frac{\partial}{\partial x^{\prime}}\quad,\quad Z_{y}^{\prime}=\frac{\partial}{\partial y^{\prime}}-m\,x^{\prime}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\quad,\quad Z_{z}^{\prime}=\frac{\partial}{\partial z^{\prime}}-m\,y^{\prime}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}^{\prime}}+m\,x^{\prime}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}^{\prime}}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{Z}^{\prime x}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}^{\prime}}\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime y}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{y}^{\prime}}\quad,\quad\tilde{Z}^{\prime z}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{z}^{\prime}}\ ,\end{gathered}

with the non-vanishing Lie brackets

(6.134)

\displaystyle[Z_{x}^{\prime},Z_{z}^{\prime}]=m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime y}\ ,\quad[Z_{x}^{\prime},Z_{y}^{\prime}]=-m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime z}\qquad\text{and}\qquad[Z_{z}^{\prime},Z_{y}^{\prime}]=m\,\tilde{Z}^{\prime x}\ ,

and dual one-forms

\displaystyle\begin{gathered}\Theta^{\prime x}=\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\quad,\quad\Theta^{\prime y}=\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\quad,\quad\Theta^{\prime z}=\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\ ,\\[4.0pt]
\tilde{\Theta}_{x}^{\prime}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{x}^{\prime}+m\,y^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\quad,\quad\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{y}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}^{\prime}-m\,x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\quad,\quad\tilde{\Theta}_{z}^{\prime}=\mathrm{d}\tilde{z}^{\prime}+m\,x^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\ .\end{gathered}

This is the global diagonalising frame for the almost para-Hermitian structure
(\eta_{2},{\mathscr{K}}_{2})
on the doubled nilmanifold
M^{\mathsf{H}}
, with
\varphi^{*}\eta_{2}=\eta_{1}
as well as

(6.137)

\displaystyle{\mathscr{K}}_{2}=Z_{i}^{\prime}\otimes\Theta^{\prime i}-\tilde{Z}^{\prime i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\omega_{2}=\Theta^{\prime i}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\ .

Note that the
+1
-eigenbundle
L_{2+}
of
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
is not an integrable distribution.

Taking the quotient by the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
with
L_{1-}=T{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
leaves the coordinates
(x,y,z)
, which by Equation (
6.3.2
) parametrise the Heisenberg nilmanifold
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
, while the quotient by the foliation
{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}
with
L_{2-}=T{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}
leaves the coordinates
(x^{\prime},y^{\prime},z^{\prime})
, which by Equation (
6.3.3
) parametrise the three-torus
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
.
Thus
M^{\mathsf{H}}
is the doubled space for
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
and
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
obtained in
[
30
, Section 7.3]
, and hence the respective quotients
M^{\mathsf{H}}/{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}={\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
and
M^{\mathsf{H}}/{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}={\mathsf{T}}^{3}
of
M^{\mathsf{H}}
by the foliations
{\mathcal{F}}_{1-}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2-}
integrating the
-1
-eigenbundles
L_{1-}
of
{\mathscr{K}}_{1}
and
L_{2-}
of
{\mathscr{K}}_{2}
are smooth manifolds. Since
{\mathsf{T}}^{{\mathsf{H}}}
and
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
can be viewed as circle bundles over
{\mathsf{T}}^{2}
, Lemma
5.8
implies that

(6.138)

\displaystyle C\coloneqq\set{(\varpi_{1}(m),\varpi_{2}(\varphi(m)))\,}{\,m\in M^{{\mathsf{H}}}}={\mathsf{T}}^{{\mathsf{H}}}\times_{{\mathsf{T}}^{2}}{\mathsf{T}}^{3}

is hence smooth.

We are now ready to apply the results of Section
6.2
.
We know that
H_{1}=0
is closed, and hence we may apply Proposition
6.87
to obtain the T-duality relation
R\colon(\mathbb{T}{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}},0)\dashrightarrow(\mathbb{T}{\mathsf{T}}^{3},\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}})
, where
\Phi=\overline{\varphi}\circ{\,\rm e}\,^{B}\,
and

(6.139)

\displaystyle\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}=m\,\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\wedge\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\wedge\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\ .

Since Lemma
6.100
says that the two-form

(6.140)

\displaystyle B=\Theta^{y}\wedge\tilde{\Theta}_{y}=(\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z)\wedge\mathrm{d}\tilde{y}

has the appropriate invariance,
26
26

26

Note that the
H
-flux
H_{2}=(\varphi^{-1})^{*}\mathrm{d}B=\varpi_{2}^{*}\,\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}
is exact on
M^{\mathsf{H}}
. However, this
B
-field is not basic and so does not descend to a two-form on
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
. On the other hand,
H_{2}
is basic and descends to a cohomologically non-trivial three-form
\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}
on
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
. That
B
is not basic is the origin of ‘topology change’ in T-duality.
the relation

R=\mathrm{Span}\set{(Z_{x},Z_{x}^{\prime})\,,\,(Z_{y},\Theta^{\prime y})\,,\,(Z_{z},Z_{z}^{\prime})\,,\,(\Theta^{x},\Theta^{\prime x})\,,\,(\Theta^{y},Z_{y}^{\prime})\,,\,(\Theta^{z},\Theta^{\prime z})}

is a generalised isometry in the following way.

The standard left-invariant Riemannian metric
{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}
on the Heisenberg nilmanifold
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
is given by

(6.141)

\displaystyle{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}=\delta_{ij}\,{\underline{\Theta}}^{i}\otimes{\underline{\Theta}}^{j}=\mathrm{d}x\otimes\mathrm{d}x+(\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z)\otimes(\mathrm{d}y-m\,x\,\mathrm{d}z)+\mathrm{d}z\otimes\mathrm{d}z\ .

With
\mathsf{Iso}(V_{1}^{+})=\mathsf{Lie}(\mathsf{T^{H}})
, the notion of
D_{1}
invariance becomes that of
\mathsf{T^{H}}
-invariance, and hence
{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}
is a
D_{1}
-invariant metric.

The pullback of
{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}
to
M_{1}
,
g_{1+}=\varpi_{1}^{*}\,{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}=\delta_{ij}\,{\Theta}^{i}\otimes{\Theta}^{j}
, defines a generalised para-Hermitian metric
{\mathcal{H}}_{1}=g_{1+}+g_{1-}
on
M^{\mathsf{H}}
given by

(6.142)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}_{1}=\delta_{ij}\,{\Theta}^{i}\otimes{\Theta}^{j}+\delta^{ij}\,\tilde{\Theta}_{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}_{j}\ .

The pullback by
\varphi^{-1}
gives
{\mathcal{H}}_{2}=\delta_{ij}\,{\Theta}^{\prime i}\otimes{\Theta}^{\prime j}+\delta^{ij}\,\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\tilde{\Theta}^{\prime}_{j}
, and hence we get a basic tensor
g_{2+}=\delta_{ij}\,{\Theta}^{\prime i}\otimes{\Theta}^{\prime j}
which is the pullback by
\varpi_{2}
of the standard flat Riemannian metric

(6.143)

\displaystyle{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}=\delta_{ij}\,{\underline{\Theta}}^{\prime i}\otimes{\underline{\Theta}}^{\prime j}=\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}+\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{d}y^{\prime}+\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}\otimes\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

on the three-torus
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
.

Thus the Heisenberg nilmanifold
{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}
with zero
H
-flux and background metric
{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}}
is T-dual to the three-torus
{\mathsf{T}}^{3}
with
H
-flux
\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}
and background metric
{\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}
via the generalised isometry

(6.144)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\colon\big(\mathbb{T}{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}\,,\,0\,,\,({\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{\mathsf{H}}},0)\big)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\big(\mathbb{T}{\mathsf{T}}^{3}\,,\,\underline{H}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}}\,,\,({\underline{g}}{}_{{\mathsf{T}}^{3}},0)\big)\ .}

Appendix A
Change of Splitting for Compositions

The composition of transverse generalised isometries
R_{1}\colon(E_{1},W_{1})\dashrightarrow(E_{2},W_{2})
and
R_{2}\colon(E_{2},W_{2})\dashrightarrow(E_{3},W_{3})
may fail due to the fact that the lifts of
W_{2}^{\pm}
for the decomposition (
4.41
) may differ for
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
.
To see the solution, let us first investigate the case of relations
R_{i}
which are graphs of classical Courant algebroid morphisms given by isomorphisms of exact Courant algebroids. Suppose that
\Phi_{1}\colon E_{1}\to E_{2}
and
\Phi_{2}\colon E_{2}\to E_{3}
are Courant algebroid isomorphisms, where
E_{i}
are exact Courant algebroids over
M_{i}
. Let
K_{i}
be isotropic involutive subbundles of
E_{i}
, let
W_{i}
be pre-
K_{i}
-transverse generalised metrics, and assume that
\Phi_{i}
is a regular transverse generalised isometry between
W_{i}
and
W_{i+1}
.

Then there exist the bundles
\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\pm}
,
\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\pm}
,
\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\prime\pm}
and
\widetilde{W}_{3}^{\prime\pm}
such that

(A.1)

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}\big(\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\pm}\big)=\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\pm}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\Phi_{2}\big(\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\prime\pm}\big)=\widetilde{W}_{3}^{\prime\pm}\ .

We can define the subbundles
\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\prime\pm}\coloneqq\Phi_{1}^{-1}\big(\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\prime\pm}\big)
. If
\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\prime\pm}
are also lifts of
W_{1}^{\pm}
to
W_{1}
, then

(A.2)

\displaystyle\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}\big(\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\prime\pm}\big)=\widetilde{W}_{3}^{\prime\pm}\ ,

and
\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}
is a regular transverse generalised isometry between
W_{1}
and
W_{3}
. If
s^{\pm}_{2}\colon W_{2}^{\pm}\to W_{2}
and
s_{2}^{\prime\pm}\colon W_{2}^{\pm}\to W_{2}
are the splittings with images
\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\pm}
and
\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\prime\pm}
respectively, the requirement that
\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\prime\pm}
are also lifts of
W_{1}^{\pm}
to
W_{1}
is that the differences
s^{\pm}_{2}-s_{2}^{\prime\pm}
map to
\Phi_{1}(K_{1})
.
Similarly one could define
\widetilde{W}_{3}^{\pm}\coloneqq\Phi_{2}\big(\widetilde{W}_{2}^{\pm}\big)
which, provided that
s^{\pm}_{2}-s_{2}^{\prime\pm}
map to
\Phi_{2}^{-1}(K_{3})
, define new splittings such that
\Phi_{2}\circ\Phi_{1}\big(\widetilde{W}_{1}^{\pm}\big)=\widetilde{W}_{3}^{\pm}
.

When
R_{i}
are general Courant algebroid relations, the condition on the splittings
s_{2}^{\pm}
and
s_{2}^{\prime\pm}
may be written as

(A.3)

\displaystyle{\rm pr}_{1}\big(R_{1}\cap(E_{1}\times\mathrm{im}(s^{\pm}_{2}-s_{2}^{\prime\pm})\big)\subset K_{1}\qquad\text{or}\qquad{\rm pr}_{2}\big(R_{2}\cap(\mathrm{im}(s_{2}^{\pm}-s_{2}^{\prime\pm})\times E_{3})\big)\subset K_{3}\ ,

which should be viewed as pointwise inclusions.
These are similar to the conditions given in Theorem
5.46
, though here the splittings
s^{\pm}_{2}
and
s_{2}^{\prime\pm}
are given
a priori
whereas in the proof of Theorem
5.46
we had to construct these splittings. That is, a portion of the proof is showing that
\Phi
is a transverse generalised isometry. The pointwise nature of the present condition is also in contrast with the construction in Section
5.2
, which provides a smooth splitting and hence shows that
\Phi
is a regular transverse generalised isometry.
